THE BUTTERFLY EFFECT

The machine stops screaming before you do.

You sit strapped in, sweating, your hands shaking on the armrests. It was a systems test. Nothing was
supposed to go anywhere. The panels are dark and the controls are dead.

You open the hatch expecting the concrete floor of the test bay. You get dirt. A mule cart rattles
past close enough to touch, and the driver shouts at you in Latin. You understand him.

Thank God I took that in college.

Ancient Rome. Not ruins. Alive.

The machine is dead weight. Its contacts and couplings are gold, and gold you can carry: you pry out
60 aurei worth of it. Every piece will have to go back before the machine could ever run again.
Rome pays in silver denarii. Gold has to be changed at the money changers (exchange <n> aurei).

Two things on this street could use your money, and you can afford only one:
  choose fountain  — The Subura's fountain runs brown. Clear the lead pipe and reline the basin.
  choose workshop  — Pay for a second forge and paid apprentices at the smith's by the Porta Trigemina, in exchange for a share of the business.
Your ears are still ringing. Before anything else you could walk and get your bearings: visit market, changers, forges, curia or subura (no Attention).
Type 'help' for commands.

== Month 1 · Ianuarius, AD 155 ==  0 denarii (+0/month) · 60 aurei (1 = 25 den.)   Index 100
  Attention: 4 free / 4 total
  Medicine      50  expect   50  (no voice)  debt     0 Stable
  Governance    55  expect   55  (no voice)  debt     0 Stable
  Economy       55  expect   55  (no voice)  debt     0 Stable
  News: Antoninus Pius has ruled for seventeen years and hasn't left Italy in all that time. People call it the quietest reign anyone can remember.   (news)
  Machine: not yet assessed, gold 0/60 aurei   (machine)
  ► Waiting: choose fountain or choose workshop (before the end of turn 8).

> choose workshop
You chose the workshop.  [Attention left this month: 2/4]

> priority medicine accept
You have no voice in Medicine yet. You need 25% of an institution that maintains it (buy into one, or found your own); until then Rome runs it without you.

> priority governance accept
You have no voice in Governance yet. You need 25% of an institution that maintains it (buy into one, or found your own); until then Rome runs it without you.

> priority economy accept
You have no voice in Economy yet. You need 25% of an institution that maintains it (buy into one, or found your own); until then Rome runs it without you.

> assess
Started: Assess the machine.  [Attention left this month: 1/4]

> repair coil
You don't know yet what's wrong with the machine. Assess it first (assess: 1 Attention a month for 2 months).

> repair coolant
You don't know yet what's wrong with the machine. Assess it first (assess: 1 Attention a month for 2 months).

> repair chronometer
You don't know yet what's wrong with the machine. Assess it first (assess: 1 Attention a month for 2 months).

> promise yes
No promise is waiting for an answer.

> respond none
There is no outbreak to respond to.

> start physician
Fund a physician for the district costs 2,250 denarii; you have 0 denarii.

> start market
Honest weights and free prices at the Forum Boarium is public business: you need 10% of an institution in Economy to push it through.

> start patronage
Patronage for a senator costs 1,875 denarii; you have 0 denarii.

> start census
Sponsor a household census is public business: you need 10% of an institution in Governance to push it through.

> start quarantine
Draft quarantine rules for Ostia's docks needs the harbor officials to listen: 10% of a Medicine or Governance institution; once the first signs of pestilence prove you right, membership in one will do.

> start magistrates
Fund the district magistrates is public business: you need 10% of an institution in Governance to push it through.

> start warehouses
Repair the Ostia warehouses costs 2,625 denarii; you have 0 denarii.

> work
You earn 125 denarii; tax takes 12 denarii, you keep 112 denarii.  [Attention left this month: 0/4]
  (No Attention left this month. Type 'end' to end the month.)

> end
  • At the small baths behind the Circus Maximus you sit in the warm room between a fuller and a man selling olives. The olive man complains that a merchant under the Aventine pays porters to bail his cellar every morning, and still the jars go green. The fuller says the merchant should sacrifice to the river and stop complaining.

  • A physician now keeps open hours in the Subura. Medicine +10.
  • A physician called Serenus finds you at the fountain, a short man with ink on his cuffs. He has heard you talk about fevers as if you'd counted them. "Have you?" he asks. You say not yet. "Neither has anyone else," he says. "Come and see my casebooks. Bring wine; my sister won't buy any."
  • You know Serenus, a member of the Physicians' Circle of the Subura.

  • Serenus spreads two years of fever cases across his sister's table: the purge, the bleeding, the cold baths, who lived. You show him how to rule the wax into columns, one case to a line, the same questions for every patient. He tries it on the last month's cases and goes quiet. "The bleeding," he says. "I bled eleven of them."

== Month 243 · Martius, AD 175 ==  19,760 denarii (+19/month) · 34 aurei (1 = 33.7 den.)   Index 121.3

  ► Serenus's invitation: invitation accept|decline circle   (institutions)
  ► Cassianus: commission look cellarpump (unpaid)   (commission)
  Machine: 9/9 repair steps, gold 60/60 aurei — ready to jump   (machine)
  ► The era's 20 years are over. Jump when you're ready (you can also stay).

> jump
You will leave AD 175 and arrive somewhere between AD 210 and AD 225: the machine's range is 35–50 years (repairs, 0 of 3 upgrades, and your time in this era). You can't come back.
What you leave behind (19,760 denarii and 34 aurei in hand):
  • No institution you hold 10%+ of will look after Rome while you're away.
  • The machine will carry you 35–50 years (repairs, 0 of 3 upgrades, and your time in this era); exactly how far, you'll know when you arrive. Upgrades and more time here would lengthen it.
  • The 19,760 denarii in your hands stay behind and are lost unless you spend them, pay down debt or endow an institution.
  • Your gold: the machine can carry 10 aurei; you hold 34 aurei, so 24 aurei would be left behind and lost (deposit <n> or bury <n>, 1 Attention each; the bank takes up to 100 aurei, a jar holds 50 aurei).
  •   Denarii can't be carried, banked or buried usefully: change them into gold first (exchange <n> denarii).
Prepare: paydown <domain> <points> · endow <inst> <denarii|all> · audit <inst> · exchange <n> denarii · deposit <aurei> · bury <aurei>. Type 'jump' again to go, or anything else to stay.

> jump

The machine shudders. Decades pass in the dark... It carries you 40 years.

— Recognition —
You climb out of the machine in AD 215. The Tiber smells the same. Near the Porta Trigemina you find
the smith's workshop you funded by its soot-black wall. It is a stable now. Down the street, the
fountain you didn't choose was filled in long ago. An old woman calls the spot 'fever corner'.

— Wrongness —
Something is wrong. The cattle market has spilled over its walls; you hear six languages haggling
over bales from half the world. The coins are lighter and paler than you remember, barely half silver
now. Prices have climbed with every cut, and soldiers grumble that their pay buys less each year.

— Personal echo —
In an old house in the Subura you find a line carved over a door, in Demetria's Greek: 'For the one
who stayed.' They don't know it means you. In the Circle's library a casebook in a careful hand sits
chained to the shelf: Serenus's fevers, set side by side, with notes in three later hands arguing
with him.

— Discovery —
You ask at the guild halls and the Curia whether anyone remembers you. No one does: you never held
enough of anything in Rome to leave your mark on it. In your purse: the 10 aurei you carried, as good
as the day you left. The gold you couldn't carry, and didn't hide or bank (24 aurei), is long gone.

You can walk around Rome: visit market, changers, forges, curia or subura (no Attention).
Type 'learn more' for the Index and what became of your institutions, 'jump' to go on another 25–40 years, or 'quit'.

(after arrival) > learn more

  Departure, AD 175: 121   Medicine 121  Governance 118  Economy 125
  Arrival,   AD 215: 117   Medicine 115  Governance 116  Economy 120

  None you held 10% or more of.
Your gold: carried 10, deposited 0 (0 back), buried 0 (0 recovered), left behind 24; you hold 10 aurei now.
Crises while you were away
  AD 189: the pestilence returns to Rome under Commodus. At its worst two thousand die in a day; the emperor goes to Laurentum for the clean air. It is contained: about 26 thousand dead, 3% of Rome.


(after arrival) > why medicine
You have been away for 40 years. What happened in between is lost to you; the world simply is what it is now. (Try 'learn more'.)

(after arrival) > quit
